=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a H \times W grid, and each cell contains 0 or 1. The cell at the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left contains an integer A_{i,j}.
You can perform the following two operations any number of times in any order:

- Operation X: Choose an integer x (1 \leq x \leq H). For every integer 1 \leq y \leq W, replace A_{x,y} with 1 - A_{x,y}.
- Operation Y: Choose an integer y (1 \leq y \leq W). For every integer 1 \leq x \leq H, replace A_{x,y} with 1 - A_{x,y}.

Find the minimum possible value of \displaystyle \sum_{x=1}^H\sum_{y=1}^W A_{x,y} after the process.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
H W
A_{1,1}A_{1,2}\ldots A_{1,W}
A_{2,1}A_{2,2}\ldots A_{2,W}
\vdots
A_{H,1}A_{H,2}\ldots A_{H,W}

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq H \leq 2\times 10^5
- 1 \leq W \leq 18
- H and W are integers.
- A_{i,1}A_{i,2}\ldots A_{i,W} is a length-W string consisting of 0 and 1.

Sample Input 1

3 3
100
010
110

Sample Output 1

2

By performing the following operations, the grid changes as shown below, and you get \displaystyle \sum_{x=1}^H\sum_{y=1}^W A_{x,y} = 2.

- Operation Y with y=1
- Operation X with x=2


It is impossible to make \displaystyle \sum_{x=1}^H\sum_{y=1}^W A_{x,y} \leq 1, so the answer is 2.

Sample Input 2

3 4
1111
1111
1111

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

10 5
10000
00111
11000
01000
10110
01110
10101
00100
00100
10001

Sample Output 3

13

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

We have H up to 2e5, W <=18. Grid binary. Operations flip entire row or entire column. Can perform any number times. Need minimize sum of ones.

We need derive algorithm.

We have H rows, each row is W-bit string. Operation X toggles row x; Operation Y toggles column y. Since toggling same row/col twice cancels, final grid = original xor row flips vector r_i for rows xor column flips vector c_j for columns. We choose binary r (H), c (W). Final bit A'_i,j = A_i,j xor r_i xor c_j. Need minimize sum over i,j of that.

Equivalent choose column flips c (length W) and row flips r. For fixed c, each row i has base row B_i = A_i xor c (bitwise). Then row flip r_i toggles all W bits; contribution of row i is min(popcount(B_i), W - popcount(B_i)) by choosing r_i = 0 or 1. Total = sum_i min(popcount(A_i xor c), W - popcount(A_i xor c)). Need minimize over c in {0,1}^W. W <=18 => 2^W up to 262144, H 2e5. Need compute for all c sum over rows of min(popcount(row xor c), W-popcount(row xor c)). Straight O(H*2^W) too high 5e10. Need optimize.

Let row bitmask a_i. Define f(c)=sum_i g(popcount(a_i xor c)), where g(k)=min(k,W-k). Need compute for all c.

This is a Walsh-Hadamard transform-like convolution. We have counts of rows by mask: cnt[m]. Then f[c] = sum_m cnt[m] * g(popcount(m xor c)). Need compute for all c. This is convolution over XOR with kernel K[d]=g(popcount(d)). f = cnt (*xor*) K. We can compute via FWHT O(W 2^W). Since W<=18, 2^W=262k, W*2^W ~4.7M good. Need implement in Python efficiently.

def fwht(a):
    h=1
    while h<n:
        for i in range(0,n,h*2):
            for j in range(i,i+h):
                x=a[j]; y=a[j+h]
                a[j]=x+y
                a[j+h]=x-y
        h*=2

def fwht(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:
        step = h << 1
        for i in range(0, n, step):
            for j in range(i, i+h):
                x = a[j]
                y = a[j+h]
                a[j] = x + y
                a[j+h] = x - y
        h = step

    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:

Let's craft code:

import sys

def fwht(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:
        step = h << 1
        for i in range(0, n, step):
            end = i + h
            for j in range(i, end):
                x = a[j]
                y = a[j + h]
                a[j] = x + y
                a[j + h] = x - y
        h = step

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    H = int(data[0]); W = int(data[1])
    n = 1 << W
    cnt = [0] * n
    for s in data[2:2+H]:
        cnt[int(s, 2)] += 1

    K = [0] * n
    for m in range(n):
        pc = m.bit_count()
        K[m] = pc if pc < W - pc else W - pc

    fwht(cnt)
    fwht(K)
    for i in range(n):
        cnt[i] *= K[i]
    fwht(cnt)

    ans = min(cnt) // n
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

def fwht(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:
        for i in range(0, n, h * 2):
            for j in range(i, i + h):
                x = a[j]
                y = a[j + h]
                a[j] = x + y
                a[j + h] = x - y
        h *= 2

Did I compute K transform or convolution orientation wrong? Let's check.

K=[0,1,1,0]. cnt at mask1. f[c]=sum_m cnt[m] K[m xor c] = K[1 xor c].
c=0: K1=1.
c=1: K0=0.
c=2: K3=0.
c=3: K2=1.
So f=[1,0,0,1]. Our inverse gave [1,0,1,0]. Something wrong in transform or multiplication? Let's recalc FWHT of K and cnt carefully.

Standard FWHT as implemented:
def fwht(a):
 h=1
 while h<n:
  for i in range(0,n,2h):
   for j in range(i,i+h):
    x=a[j]; y=a[j+h]
    a[j]=x+y; a[j+h]=x-y
  h*=2

For n=4, cnt=[0,1,0,0].
h=1:
i=0: j=0: x=0,y=1 -> a0=1,a1=-1.
i=2: j=2: x=0,y=0 -> a2=0,a3=0.
=> [1,-1,0,0].
h=2:
i=0: j=0: x=a0=1,y=a2=0 -> a0=1,a2=1.
j=1: x=a1=-1,y=a3=0 -> a1=-1,a3=-1.
=> [1,-1,1,-1]. Correct.

K=[0,1,1,0].
h=1:
i=0: j=0: x=0,y=1 -> a0=1,a1=-1.
i=2: j=2: x=1,y=0 -> a2=1,a3=1? Wait x=a2=1, y=a3=0, so a2=1, a3=1. I earlier said [1,-1,1,-1] but actually a3=1, not -1. Let's recalc: K indices: a0=0,a1=1,a2=1,a3=0.
Pair (0,1): a0=1,a1=-1.
Pair (2,3): a2=1+0=1, a3=1-0=1.
So after h=1: [1,-1,1,1].
h=2:
i=0: j=0: x=a0=1,y=a2=1 -> a0=2,a2=0.
j=1: x=a1=-1,y=a3=1 -> a1=0,a3=-2.
=> [2,0,0,-2]. Not [2,-2,0,0]. Good.

Product cnt transform [1,-1,1,-1] * K transform [2,0,0,-2] = [2,0,0,2].
Inverse FWHT [2,0,0,2]:
h=1:
pair (0,1): a0=2,a1=-2.
pair (2,3): a2=0,a3=-2? Wait x=a2=0,y=a3=2 -> a2=2,a3=-2. So after h=1: [2,-2,2,-2].
h=2:
i=0: j=0: x=2,y=2 -> a0=4,a2=0.
j=1: x=-2,y=-2 -> a1=-4,a3=0.
=> [4,-4,0,0]. Divide 4 => [1,-1,0,0]? That's not f either. Something still wrong? Let's check product/inverse maybe orientation or transform matrix.

Wait convolution f[c] = sum_m cnt[m] K[m xor c]. For cnt at 1, K[1 xor c] = [K1=1, K0=0, K3=0, K2=1] = [1,0,0,1]. We expect [1,0,0,1]. Our inverse gave [1,-1,0,0]? That seems wrong. Did I multiply wrong? Let's recalc FWHT of cnt and K with correct transform.

Maybe the standard FWHT as implemented computes a different transform? Let's derive.

Hadamard transform H_n defined recursively H_1=[1], H_{2n} = [[H_n, H_n],[H_n, -H_n]]. For n=2, H_2 = [[1,1],[1,-1]]. Applying to vector [a0,a1] gives [a0+a1, a0-a1]. Good.

For n=4, H_4 = [[H2,H2],[H2,-H2]]:
Row0: [1,1,1,1]
Row1: [1,-1,1,-1]
Row2: [1,1,-1,-1]
Row3: [1,-1,-1,1]
Our algorithm h=1 then h=2 should produce H_4 * a? Let's test a=[0,1,0,0]. H*a = [1,-1,1,-1]. We got [1,-1,1,-1]. Good.

K=[0,1,1,0]. H*K = [2,0,0,-2]? Let's compute:
row0: 0+1+1+0=2.
row1: 0-1+1-0=0.
row2: 0+1-1-0=0.
row3: 0-1-1+0=-2. Yes [2,0,0,-2]. We got [2,0,0,-2]. Good.

Product P = [2,0,0,-2]? Wait cnt transform [1,-1,1,-1] times K transform [2,0,0,-2] = [2,0,0,2]? Because -1 * -2 = 2. Yes P=[2,0,0,2].

Inverse H*P /4. H*P:
row0: 2+0+0+2=4.
row1: 2-0+0-2=0.
row2: 2+0-0-2=0.
row3: 2-0+0+2=4? Wait row3 [1,-1,-1,1] dot [2,0,0,2] = 2+0+0+2=4. So H*P = [4,0,0,4]. Divide 4 = [1,0,0,1]. Our manual inverse algorithm gave [4,-4,0,0]? Let's recalc inverse algorithm on P=[2,0,0,2].

Apply fwht to P=[2,0,0,2].
h=1:
i=0: j=0: x=2,y=0 -> a0=2,a1=2.
i=2: j=2: x=0,y=2 -> a2=2,a3=-2.
After h=1: [2,2,2,-2]. I earlier mistakenly pair (2,3): x=0,y=2 -> a2=2,a3=-2 yes.
h=2:
i=0: j=0: x=a0=2,y=a2=2 -> a0=4,a2=0.
j=1: x=a1=2,y=a3=-2 -> a1=0,a3=4.
After h=2: [4,0,0,4]. Divide 4 => [1,0,0,1]. Good. My earlier inverse mistake. So algorithm correct.

Great.

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys


def fwht(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:
        step = h << 1
        for i in range(0, n, step):
            for j in range(i, i + h):
                x = a[j]
                y = a[j + h]
                a[j] = x + y
                a[j + h] = x - y
        h = step


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    H = int(data[0])
    W = int(data[1])

    n = 1 << W
    cnt = [0] * n

    for idx in range(2, 2 + H):
        cnt[int(data[idx], 2)] += 1

    # K[d] = min(popcount(d), W - popcount(d))
    K = [0] * n
    for mask in range(n):
        pc = mask.bit_count()
        K[mask] = pc if pc < W - pc else W - pc

    fwht(cnt)
    fwht(K)

    for i in range(n):
        cnt[i] *= K[i]

    fwht(cnt)

    ans = min(cnt) // n
    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
